# Keystrand architecture and API contract

This contract defines Keystrand's Swift targets, public types, dependencies, and
rules for contributors. `docs/DESIGN.md` takes precedence for visual and
structural decisions. Section 11 contains the public API reference generated
by `scripts/make-api-reference.sh`. If this contract conflicts with the code,
the code takes precedence.

## 1. Package and project layout

`Package.swift` defines the SwiftPM package `KeystrandKit`. It uses tools 6.2,
`.macOS(.v26)`, and `swiftLanguageModes: [.v6]`. The package has no third-party
dependencies. It sets no `defaultIsolation`, so targets use nonisolated
inference. UI types declare `@MainActor` explicitly, as described in section 4.

`project.yml` defines the XcodeGen project with `SWIFT_VERSION` 6.0 and a macOS
26.0 deployment target. It defines one app target, `Keystrand`, with the synced
source folder `Keystrand/` and a dependency on the local `AppShell` product.
XcodeGen writes `Config/Info.plist`, which registers the `keystrand` URL scheme
through `CFBundleURLTypes`. The `Keystrand` scheme builds the app. Its Test
action runs all 17 package test targets.

The project disables App Sandbox and the hardened runtime. It uses ad hoc
signing with identity `-`. The app copies `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`,
and `LICENSES/` into `Contents/Resources` with `buildPhase: resources`.
**Help > Acknowledgements** displays them. `scripts/install.sh` checks that
the built app contains them.

`Keystrand/App/KeystrandApp.swift` declares `@main` and shows `RootScene()`.
It installs `AppShellDelegate` through `@NSApplicationDelegateAdaptor`.
The delegate waits at most 2 seconds for open sessions to close before quit.

The app resources contain the following assets:

- `Keystrand/Resources/Assets.xcassets` contains AppIcon, drawn by
  `scripts/make-app-icon.swift`.
- The same catalog contains the indigo AccentColor, with `#4A55C7` for light
  appearance and `#7480F0` for dark appearance. macOS uses it only if the user's
  accent setting is Multicolor.
- `Keystrand/Resources/Credits.rtf` contains the copyright, trademark, and
  acknowledgements text for the standard About panel.

### Targets and dependencies

| Target | Path | Depends on | Resources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RESP | Sources/RESP | none | none |
| RedisClient | Sources/RedisClient | RESP | none |
| Formatters | Sources/Formatters | RESP | none |
| CommandSpecs | Sources/CommandSpecs | RESP | `.copy("Resources/Commands")`: redis, redisearch, redisjson, redistimeseries, redisbloom, redisgraph `.json` (third-party data, see `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`) and a `README.md` the loader ignores. `redis.json` and the module files are written by `scripts/make-redis-commands.py` from the pins in `scripts/command-sources.lock`; the loader types expression blocks such as FT.AGGREGATE APPLY as strings |
| RedisCore | Sources/RedisCore | RESP, RedisClient, Formatters, CommandSpecs (all `@_exported`) | none |
| RedisUI | Sources/RedisUI | RedisCore (`@_exported`) | none |
| ConnectionsFeature | Sources/Features/ConnectionsFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | none |
| BrowserFeature | Sources/Features/BrowserFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | `.copy("Resources/SampleData")` (common, json, search, timeseries, bloom, vectorset, manifest.json), generated by `scripts/make-sample-data.py` |
| KeyTypesBasicFeature | Sources/Features/KeyTypesBasicFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | none |
| KeyTypesAdvancedFeature | Sources/Features/KeyTypesAdvancedFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | none |
| WorkbenchFeature | Sources/Features/WorkbenchFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | `.copy("Resources/Tutorials")` (manifest.json and 12 original Markdown pages in `ds/`, `sq/`, `keystrand/`) |
| CLIFeature | Sources/Features/CLIFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | none |
| AnalyticsFeature | Sources/Features/AnalyticsFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | `.process("Resources")` (recommendations.json) |
| MonitoringFeature | Sources/Features/MonitoringFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | none |
| VectorSearchFeature | Sources/Features/VectorSearchFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | `.copy("Resources/SampleData")` (vector-search-sample-data.json, trails, recipes), generated by `scripts/make-sample-data.py` |
| SettingsFeature | Sources/Features/SettingsFeature | RedisCore, RedisUI | none |
| AppShell | Sources/AppShell | RedisCore, RedisUI, all 10 features | none |
| TestSupport (library) | Tests/TestSupport | RedisCore | none |
| `<X>Tests` (16 test targets) | Tests/`<X>`Tests | X, TestSupport | none |
| AppShellTests | Tests/AppShellTests | AppShell, RedisCore, ConnectionsFeature, SettingsFeature, TestSupport | none |

Test targets are RESPTests, RedisClientTests, FormattersTests, CommandSpecsTests, RedisCoreTests, RedisUITests, and one
`<Feature>Tests` per feature, for example `BrowserFeatureTests`, plus `AppShellTests` (layout math, window connection lifecycle, deep-link redirects). Products are `AppShell` and `RedisCore`.

A feature imports `RedisUI`, which re-exports `RedisCore` and therefore RESP, RedisClient, Formatters,
and CommandSpecs. Features never import each other. Access bundled resources with `Bundle.module` inside
the owning target.

### Constraints

- **Toolchain.** Xcode 27 (Swift 6.4 toolchain), Swift 6 language mode with strict concurrency. The build has zero
  errors and zero warnings.
- **Platform.** Deployment target macOS 26.0. macOS 26 APIs (Liquid Glass, `glassEffect`, `GlassEffectContainer`,
  `ToolbarSpacer`, `backgroundExtensionEffect`, `scrollEdgeEffectStyle`) are used directly, with no `#available`
  fallbacks.
- **No third-party packages** (decision 1).
- **Project generation.** `xcodegen` generates `Keystrand.xcodeproj` and `Config/Info.plist` from `project.yml`; both
  are git-ignored. The app target is a synced folder, so new files need no regenerate. Regenerate only when
  `project.yml` changes.
- **Distribution.** App Sandbox and hardened runtime are off; signing is ad-hoc (`CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY: "-"`), and
  `scripts/install.sh` re-signs the Release build with a local identity when one is available. The app spawns
  `/usr/bin/ssh` for SSH tunnels and the user's login shell for connection scripts.

### Conventions

- **Binary-safe data.** Key names and values are `Data` at the protocol layer; display layers convert them with the
  chosen formatter. Keys that are not valid UTF-8 must still list and open.
- **Errors.** Typed errors (`enum XxxError: Error, LocalizedError`) with user-readable messages. Never crash on bad
  server input. No force unwraps, `try!` or `as!` outside tests.
- **Vocabulary.** UI text and code use the established Redis-tool names: Browser, Workbench, Key, TTL, Database,
  Slow Log, Pub/Sub, Profiler, Bulk Actions, Command Helper.
- **UI.** Every screen, component, color, font and idiom follows `docs/DESIGN.md`.
- **Comments** only where the code is not self-explanatory.

## 2. Recorded decisions

1. **Apple frameworks only.** Do not add third-party packages. The code uses
   Foundation, Network (NWConnection and TLS), Security (Keychain), Compression,
   Synchronization, SwiftUI, AppKit (`NSViewRepresentable` wrappers), and Swift
   Testing. Swift Charts renders time series, donut, and bar charts. MapKit
   renders Workbench geo maps. Add `import Charts` or `import MapKit` in the
   feature target without changing `Package.swift`.
2. **Type-specific value services live in the feature that uses them.** Examples: HashService,
   ListService, SetService, and StringService in KeyTypesBasicFeature; ZSet, Stream, JSON, VectorSet, and
   Array services in KeyTypesAdvancedFeature; DatabaseAnalyzer, AnalysisAggregator, DatabaseAnalysisStore,
   SlowLogService, and ClusterDetailsService in AnalyticsFeature; CLISession in CLIFeature;
   ConnectionURLParser, LocalAutoDiscovery, SentinelDiscovery UI flow, and RedisSoftwareDiscovery in
   ConnectionsFeature; KeyTreeBuilder and search-history logic in BrowserFeature. RedisCore keeps only
   what two or more targets share, so a feature change rarely touches a shared target. Build these services on
   `session.execute`, `session.pipeline`, and `session.nodes`.
3. **Names.** Each concept has one name in code. The left column lists other names for the same concept (a few
   remain as typealiases); use the right column:

| Other name(s) | Code |
| --- | --- |
| `DatabaseConnection`, Database model | `DatabaseConfig` (RedisCore) |
| `RedisKeyType`, `KeyType` | `KeyType` (`typealias RedisKeyType = KeyType`) |
| `Endpoint` | `RedisEndpoint` (RedisClient) |
| `RedisModule`, `RedisModuleInfo` | `RedisModuleInfo` |
| `ServerCapabilities`, `DatabaseCapabilities` | `DatabaseCapabilities` (`session.capabilities`) |
| `KeyValueFormat`, `ValueFormat` | `ValueFormat` (Formatters) |
| `ValueCompressor`, `Compressor` | `Compressor` (Formatters, includes `.none`) |
| `FormattedValue`, `FormatResult` | `FormatResult` (`typealias FormattedValue`) |
| `DatabaseUIConfigStore`, `BrowserDatabaseConfig`, `DatabaseSettings` | `DatabaseSettings` + `DatabaseSettingsStore` (RedisCore) |
| `BrowserGlobalPrefs`, `BrowserSettings.viewFormat` | `settings.browser` (`BrowserGlobalPrefs`) |
| `UIPreferences`, `AppSettings` | `settings.ui`, `settings.app` on `SettingsStore` |
| `RedisConnectionConfig`, `ConnectionConfig` | `RedisConnectionConfig` (RedisClient) |
| `RedisError` | `RedisClientError` (`.server(RESPError)` for error replies) |
| `WorkbenchExecutor`, `CommandExecutor` | `CommandExecutor` (RedisCore, `type: .workbench / .search`) |
| `CommandExecutionStore`, `CommandHistoryStore` | `CommandHistoryStore` |
| `SearchIndexCatalog`, `KeySearchService`, `SearchIndexService` | `SearchIndexService` |
| `CommandSpecStore` | `CommandCatalog` (`CommandCatalog.shared`) |
| `ArgumentSyntax` | `CommandSyntax` |
| `RunQueryMode`, `ReplyNode` | defined in **Formatters** (`ReplyNodeBuilder` needs them), re-exported |
| `ToastCenter`, `Toast` | error queue in **RedisCore**; presented by AppShell `.shellStatusAlerts` (one alert at a time) and inside sheets by RedisUI `.sheetAlerts` |
| `BulkActionsService` | `KeyOperations.bulkDelete` / `.bulkUpload` (RedisCore) |
| `CommandFileImporter` | `CommandFileImporter` (RedisCore) |
| `ConnectionKind` | `ConnectionKind` (`session.connectionKind`) |

1. **Error replies.** `RedisClient.execute` throws `RedisClientError.server(RESPError)` when Redis answers
   with an error reply. `pipeline` returns error replies in place as `RESPValue.error` and throws only on
   transport failure. `client.send(_:)` returns one reply without throwing on `-ERR` (CLI and Workbench).
2. **Secrets.** Secrets never go into JSON. `DatabaseStore` writes `withoutSecrets()` and stores the
   password, sentinel password, SSH password/key/passphrase, and client cert key in the `SecretStore`
   (Keychain service `Keystrand`, accounts per `SecretAccount`). `DatabaseStore.list()` returns no
   secrets. `get(id)` fills them in.
3. **Persistence.** All persistence is JSON under `~/Library/Application Support/Keystrand`
   (`AppPaths.default`): `databases.json`, `certificates.json`, `settings.json`,
   `database-settings/<id>.json`, `workbench/<id>[-search].json`, `QueryLibrary.json`,
   `Analysis/<id>/<report>.json`, and `ProfilerLogs/`. No encryption. Deletions go to the Trash
   (`JSONFile.discard`). Every store file carries a `schemaVersion`; a file without one is version 1.
   List files are `{"schemaVersion": n, "<list>": [...]}` (`databases`, `items`) and still read the
   bare array they replaced. `settings.json` and `database-settings/<id>.json` keep their object and
   add the key beside its fields. A file from a newer version is copied to `<name>.v<n>-<time>.json`
   and still loaded; an unreadable file, or one whose entries or sections fail to decode, is copied
   to `<name>.damaged-<time>.json`. The next save writes the current version.
4. **Window model.** `SessionRegistry` keeps one `DatabaseSession` per database ID.
   `WindowGroup(for: DatabaseID.self)` focuses an existing window for the same id. The session closes
   only when its NSWindow really closes (`NSWindow.willCloseNotification`, observed by `.onWindowClose`), never on
   minimize, hide, or tab merge. `.onDisappear` only pauses overview polling (`windowDidDisappear`). Quitting the app
   closes every open session through `AppShellDelegate.applicationShouldTerminate`, which also waits for sessions that
   are still closing (`SessionRegistry.closeAll()`). A second `SessionRegistry.close(_:)` for the same id waits for the
   first one. AppShell's `LastWindowCloseGuard` takes each window's delegate role, forwards every message to SwiftUI's
   delegate, and answers `windowShouldClose` itself to confirm closing the last window while a database is connected.
5. **Feature-specific UI state that must survive section switches** lives in
   `session.scopedObject(MyViewModel.self) { MyViewModel(...) }`. Examples are the Pub/Sub and Profiler
   view models, the CLI output buffer, and Command Helper state. Conform to `SessionScopedObject` to
   clean up (stop MONITOR, unsubscribe) when the window closes. With this, AppShell does not need to
   know feature view-model types.
6. **Result cards.** Workbench and Vector Search each own their result cards. Workbench owns the full
   visualizations. Vector Search can render text/table results with RedisUI pieces. Some duplication is
   acceptable.
7. **Key details composition.** AppShell builds `VStack { KeyDetailsHeaderView(context:) ; <type view> }`
    and injects it into `BrowserView(keyDetails:)`. The header is a single implementation in
    KeyTypesBasicFeature that renders every type's labels from `KeyInfo` (Entries, Top-level values,
    Samples, Nodes, Quant type, Vector dim, Count). Type views render only their body and subheader. The
    shared subheader building blocks (`ValueFormatPicker`, `KeySubheaderBar`) are in RedisUI.
8. **Theme and component kit (DESIGN.md sections 4 to 7).** Use only semantic
   system colors and the user's accent color. `Sources` must not define hex
   colors, palettes, or `Theme` tokens. `KeyType.tint` and `ChartPalette` map
   to system colors. Editors, the CLI and the Profiler take colors and fonts from `EditorAppearance`
   (dynamic `NSColor`s, monospaced system font). The appearance override is only `ThemeApplier` (`NSApp.appearance`)
   through `View.appAppearance(_:)`; the `--appearance` flag wins over the saved setting for one run. Errors from
   `ToastCenter` appear through AppShell's `.shellStatusAlerts(_:)`, one alert
   at a time. Do not show transient status messages (DESIGN.md section 4.3).
   Present confirmations through `.destructiveConfirmation` and
   `.productionConfirmations`, using alerts or confirmation dialogs. A sheet presents its session's confirmations and errors itself with `.sheetAlerts(session)`.
   RedisUI exposes only the DESIGN.md §4 kit.
9. **Sheets** are built on RedisUI `SheetForm` (title, content, footer with optional leading actions, Cancel, primary)
   and sized with `.sheetSize(.small | .form | .large)`. Features do not hand-build sheet footers or keep local sheet
   wrappers.
10. **Number formatting.** Counts use one grouping style app-wide: `NumberFormatting.grouped` (Formatters) writes
    `2,016`, comma-grouped and independent of the user's locale so tables, status bars, tooltips and tests agree.
    Feature helpers (`AnalyticsFormat.number`, `OverviewFormatting.grouped`, `BrowserFormatting.grouped`) forward to
    it or use the same en-US style. The toolbar overview text omits CPU until its second sample gives a value, and
    `ViewThatFits` drops clients, keys, ops/s and then CPU when the activity area is narrow.
11. **Section routing.** Switch a section's pages in place within its view.
    Vector Search uses this pattern for its index list, query page, and Create
    Index page. A pushed `NavigationStack` page hides the `.primaryAction`
    items that AppShell declares after section content.
12. **Menu shortcuts own key equivalents.** A key equivalent that a menu item carries (for example ⌘↩ Run Commands,
    owned by Database > Run Commands through `editorRunAction`) is not repeated on a toolbar button; the toolbar
    button shows the shortcut only in its help text. In-view `.keyboardShortcut`s are limited to the cases listed in
    DESIGN.md §8.8.
13. **Connection scripts.** `RedisClientFactory` runs the pre-connection script
    before each connection it opens. The post-disconnect script runs once per
    connection lifetime, rather than per socket. A teardown script can close a
    shared tunnel, which breaks other connections.

    `DatabaseFactory.test`, `createDetected`, and `sentinelMasters` close all
    connections they open before they run the post-disconnect script. They also
    run it if the operation fails. They return only after the script exits.

    `DatabaseSession` runs the script in `disconnect()` and `close()` if it
    opened or tried to open a connection after the previous script run.
    If `apply(config:)` changes the connection, it runs the previous config's
    script. New session connections wait for any running post-disconnect script.
    Setup and teardown therefore never overlap.

    `RedisConnection.close()` waits for Network.framework to report socket
    cancellation, with a 2-second limit. It also waits for its SSH tunnels to exit.

14. **Server flavor.** Valkey reports the compatibility value
    `redis_version:7.2.4`. `DatabaseCapabilities` therefore stores `serverFlavor`
    and the actual `serverVersion` alongside `redisVersion`.
    `RedisInfo.serverVersion` reads `valkey_version` on Valkey.

    Use `isVersion(atLeast:)` only for features Redis supported by 7.2.4.
    It compares the compatibility version. For newer features, use
    `isRedis(atLeast:)`, `isValkey(atLeast:)`, or a named capability such as
    `supportsHashFieldTTL`, `searchFlavor`, or `isRediSearch(below:)`.
    The flags `supportsSearchMatchAll`, `supportsSearchExplain`, and
    `searchIndexesRequireDB0` are false only on valkey-search.

    CommandSpecs `ModuleNames` defines module names. Module panels call
    `capabilities.missingModule(for:)`. It maps valkey-bloom's `bf` to
    `CommandModuleRequirement.bloomFilterOnlyModule`. The commands under `CF.`,
    `CMS.`, `TOPK.`, and `TDIGEST.` therefore remain unavailable.

    Format versions with `DatabaseCapabilities.displayName(flavor:version:)`.
    Format product names with `ServerFlavor.displayName`. Before `INFO` loads,
    `session.serverFlavor` uses the saved provider.

    Workbench, CLI Tab, and Vector Search completion lists use
    `capabilities.commandAvailability`, a CommandSpecs `CommandAvailability`.
    Completion applies these rules:

    - Module commands require their provider and `CommandModuleRequirement`.
    - Vector set commands stay hidden if the server is known to lack them.
    - Valkey-only core commands `DELIFEQ`, `CLUSTERSCAN`, and `COMMANDLOG` stay
      hidden if the server is known to be Redis.
    - `FT.EXPLAIN` and `FT.PROFILE` stay hidden on valkey-search.
    - Before Keystrand identifies the server, completion offers every command.

15. **Numbered databases in cluster mode.** Valkey 9 clusters support databases
    in `0..<cluster-databases`. Redis and older Valkey clusters support only
    database 0. `session.dbIndex` records the selected database.
    `session.effectiveDB` records the database that commands use.

    `effectiveDB` uses `dbIndex` unless the cluster cannot select it. It falls
    back to 0 if server details are unavailable, the server is Redis or Valkey
    before 9, or `dbIndex >= cluster-databases`. Every reader or writer of a
    database index must use `effectiveDB`. This includes history, keyspace
    totals, scans, the CLI prompt, the picker, and live tips.

    `connect()` opens the cluster on the last known `effectiveDB`, initially 0.
    It loads server details. If `capabilities.supportsClusterDatabases` is true,
    it reads `CONFIG GET cluster-databases` into `databaseCount`.
    If `effectiveDB` changes, it drops the old pooled client, opens a client on
    the new database, and increments `dbGeneration`.

    The `cluster-databases` count persists across reconnects to the same server.
    `apply(config:)` clears it when the connection changes. Other clusters
    report `databaseCount == 1`. The toolbar and menu database pickers therefore
    appear only for Valkey 9 clusters with more than one database.

    Descriptors carry `clusterDatabases` after Keystrand loads the count or a
    cluster caller resolves a nonzero database from `effectiveDB`. Without that
    value, `RedisClientFactory` forces database 0 for connection tests and type
    detection. `ClusterClient` uses `nodeConfig.dbIndex` unchanged.
    Every node connection runs `SELECT` during its handshake, including later
    connections for `MOVED` or `ASK`. Dedicated CLI, Pub/Sub, and `MONITOR`
    connections also select that database.

    `MOVED` replies contain no database index. `SELECT n` fails with
    `ERR DB index is out of range` if `n >= cluster-databases`.
    `select(db:)` checks the cluster count and increments `dbGeneration`, as it
    does on standalone servers. The CLI keeps its own database index.
    It starts with its first connection's index and updates it after each
    successful `SELECT`. Redirect and reconnect connections select that index.

## 4. Threading rules

Use Swift 6 language mode with complete concurrency checking. The build must
have no errors or concurrency warnings. Apply these rules:

- Use Swift concurrency. Do not use Combine. Use GCD only where AppKit or
  Network.framework requires it.
- Guard shared mutable state in non-actor classes with `Mutex` from
  Synchronization. Every `@unchecked Sendable` and `nonisolated(unsafe)` needs
  a one-line comment that explains why it is safe.
- Do not set `.defaultIsolation(MainActor.self)` on UI targets. Their pure
  parsers, aggregators, and models run off the main actor through `offMain`,
  `Task.detached`, and task groups. Declare `@MainActor` on view models,
  stores, and services that need it.
- Declare pure static helpers on a `View` or other main-actor type as `nonisolated`, such as
  `DataGridView.looksLikeJSON`. Tests and background code can then call them.
  Swift Testing's `#expect` does not flag main-actor calls from nonisolated
  tests. Those calls trap at runtime.
- Label the second closure if a function has two defaulted closures, such as
  `CommandExecutor.run(onPersistError:onChunk:)`. Swift 6 binds an unlabeled
  trailing closure to the first closure parameter.
- Use actors for connections and stores. Actors include `RedisConnection`,
  `ClusterClient`, `SSHTunnel`, `ClientPool`, `DatabaseStore`, `CertificateStore`,
  `DatabaseSettingsStore`, `CommandHistoryStore`, `QueryLibraryStore`,
  `DatabaseOverviewService`, `DangerousCommandsProvider`, and `MockRedisClient`.
- Declare UI-facing models as `@MainActor @Observable final class`. This applies
  to `DatabaseSession`, `SessionRegistry`, `AppServices`, `SettingsStore`,
  `DatabaseNavigator`, `ToastCenter`, `ConfirmationCenter`, `KeyDetailsState`,
  and every feature view model.
- Declare session-bound services as `@MainActor struct`. `KeyScanner`,
  `KeyMetadataService`, `KeyOperations`, `SearchIndexService`, `CommandExecutor`,
  and `CommandFileImporter` wrap a session and suspend on client actors for I/O.
- Run CPU-heavy parsing, tree building, and analysis aggregation in
  `nonisolated` static functions or `Task.detached`. Publish the results on
  the main actor.
- Use value types or caseless enums for pure code. RESP, Formatters,
  CommandSpecs, and parsers are `Sendable`.
- Use `AsyncStream` or `AsyncThrowingStream` for Pub/Sub, `MONITOR`, bulk delete,
  and bulk import. Cancel the consuming `Task` to stop work through `onTermination`.
- Use typed errors, such as `enum XxxError: Error, LocalizedError`.
  Display them with `session.toasts.error(error)` or inline. Invalid server
  input must not crash the app.

## 5. How code gets what it needs

### Services and data directory

AppShell injects `AppServices.shared` with `.environment(services)`.
It injects settings with `.environment(services.settings)`. Views read these
values with `@Environment(AppServices.self)` and `@Environment(SettingsStore.self)`.
View models with a session can use `session.services`.
Tests and previews use `AppServices(paths: .temporary(), secrets: InMemorySecretStore())`
or `AppServices.preview()`.

If `$KEYSTRAND_DATA_DIR` is set and nonblank, `AppPaths.default` uses its
tilde-expanded value as the data root. Otherwise it uses
`~/Library/Application Support/Keystrand`. `AppServices.shared` uses that root.
Secrets remain in Keychain.

### Sessions and commands

Pass sessions as parameters. Do not put them in the environment. AppShell gets
a session through `try await services.sessions.session(for: id)`. It passes
the session to each section view as `init(session:)`.

For tests and previews, use `DatabaseSession(config:services:client:)` with
`MockRedisClient`. `RedisTestServer.mockSession(...)` creates this setup.

Use these session methods for commands and connections:

- `session.execute(["GET", .data(key)])` sends one command.
- `session.execute(args as [Data])` sends a `[Data]` command.
- `session.pipeline(...)` sends a batch.
- `session.nodes(role: .primary)` supports work on each node. Standalone servers
  return one entry.
- `session.makeDedicatedConnection()` and `makeDedicatedNodeConnections()` open
  dedicated connections for Pub/Sub, `MONITOR`, and CLI.
- `session.client(context: .cli)` returns a pooled client for that context.

The pool keys clients by database, context, and logical database index.
`select(db:)` therefore changes the client automatically. Watch
`session.dbGeneration` to reload after a logical database switch.

### Settings

Read shared settings through the following properties:

| Setting | Property |
| --- | --- |
| Theme | `settings.app.theme` |
| Date format | `settings.app.dateFormat` / `formatOptions` |
| Scan threshold | `settings.app.effectiveScanThreshold` |
| Workbench batch size, cleanup, and modes | `settings.app.workbench*` |
| Auto-refresh rates by postfix | `settings.ui.autoRefreshRates` |
| CLI history | `settings.ui.cliInputHistory` / `pushCLIHistory` |
| Home sorting | `settings.ui.homeSorting` |
| Browser view type, search mode, value format, and column widths | `settings.browser` |

Read or update per-database settings with
`services.databaseSettingsStore.settings(for:)` and `update(_:_:)`.
These settings include tree delimiters, sort order, prefix length, visible
columns, Slow Log units, hidden tips, tutorial confirmation, search history,
and `featureData` blobs.

### Navigation

Use `session.navigator.navigate(...)` with one of these destinations:

- `.workbench(script:)`.
- `.browser(BrowserNavigationRequest)`.
- `.browseIndex`, `.vectorSearchQuery`, or `.vectorSearchCreateIndex`.
- `.pubSub` or `.analytics(tab)`.
- `.bottomPanel(tool)` or `.tutorial(id:)`.
- `.home` or `.editDatabase(focusEnvironment:)`.

AppShell switches sections from `navigator.section`. The destination consumes
its payload in `.onAppear` and `.onChange(of: navigator.pending?.id)`.
Use `navigator.consume { if case .browser = $0 { true } else { false } }`
for a Browser request. Watch `navigator.refreshGeneration` for ⌘R.

### Production confirmations

Before a destructive write, call
`await session.confirm(ConfirmationRequest(title:, message:, actionID:))`.
In key views, call `context.confirmProduction(_:)`. The call returns true
immediately unless the database environment is production.

AppShell applies `.productionConfirmations(session.confirmations)` at the window
root. macOS hides window dialogs while a sheet is open. If a `.sheet` can call
`session.confirm`, `confirmProduction`, or `session.toasts.error`, apply
`.sheetAlerts(session)` to its root. The innermost sheet presents the dialog.
The window root waits.

`requiresTyping: true` is the default for bulk delete and dangerous Workbench,
CLI, or Vector Search commands. It shows a critical alert with a field for the
database name and a Redis ACL tip. `context.confirmProduction` sets
`requiresTyping = false`. It shows a confirmation dialog with
"Don't ask again this session". `message` supports inline Markdown, including
`**bold**`. Session skips reset when the logical database changes.

### Errors and status

Report a failed user action with `session.toasts.error(error)`. Home uses
`services.toasts`. AppShell presents one error alert at a time. Each error
stays queued until the user dismisses it. An app-wide error from
`services.toasts` opens Home or brings it forward to display the alert.

If a successful action has a visible result, show no status message.
Otherwise, show its result inline in the sheet, popover, or bar that started
the action. Use `SheetForm(successMessage:)` or `InlineMessage`.
Do not show transient status messages, as specified in `DESIGN.md` section 4.3.
Show background polling errors inline in the feature.

### Key details

The Browser composes key details through these interfaces:

- BrowserFeature creates
  `KeyDetailsContext(session:key:type:state:onKeyRenamed:onKeyDeleted:onKeyChanged:onClose:onToggleFullScreen:refreshKeyInfo:)`
  with its own `KeyDetailsState`. It calls the injected `keyDetails(context)`.
- The header writes `state.info` and `state.isFullScreen`. It calls
  `state.requestRefresh()` for manual and automatic refresh.
- Type views reload on `.onChange(of: context.state.refreshGeneration)`.
  They set `state.isRefreshDisabled` while editing. They call `onKeyDeleted()`
  after removing the last element.
- AppShell injects `VectorSetAddKeyForm(context: AddKeyFormContext)` through
  `BrowserView(addKeyForm:)` for the Vector Set Add Key section.

### Windows and database changes

AppShell declares the following scenes:

- The Home library uses `Window("Redis Databases", id: "home")`, hosts
  `HomeView`, and opens with ⌘0.
- Each database uses `WindowGroup("Database", for: DatabaseID.self)`.
  Its default size is 1320×860. Its `contentMinSize` is 900×600.
- Settings uses the Settings scene.
- Keyboard Shortcuts uses fixed `Window("Keyboard Shortcuts", id: "shortcuts")`
  and opens with ⌘/.
- Acknowledgements uses `Window("Acknowledgements", id: "acknowledgements")`.
  **Help > Acknowledgements** opens SettingsFeature's `AcknowledgementsView`.
  It renders `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, `LICENSE`, and `LICENSES/*.txt`
  from `Bundle.main`.

`HomeView` calls `onOpenDatabase(config)`. AppShell calls
`openWindow(value: config.id)`. Databases links and `navigate(.home)` bring
Home forward through `HomeWindowActivator`.

**File > Add Database…** (⌘N) and **File > Import Connections…** (⇧⌘I) send
`HomeCommandCenter.shared.send(_:)`. Home consumes the request and opens its
sheet. ⇧⌘N opens **Key > New Key…**.

To delete databases, Connections calls `services.deleteDatabases(ids)` before
`onDatabasesDeleted(ids)`. AppShell dismisses their windows.
`services.saveDatabase(config)` saves a database, drops pooled clients, and
updates its open session. The production prompt and **Database > Edit Database…**
(⌥⌘E) open `DatabaseFormView(mode: .edit(config), focusEnvironment:)` in the
database window.

Database windows restore with their `DatabaseID` and reconnect.
`@SceneStorage` restores sidebar selection, inspector pane and visibility,
and bottom panel visibility.

### Connection links

`keystrand://databases/connect?…` links arrive through `.onOpenURL` in any
window. AppShell brings Home forward and sends
`HomeCommandCenter.shared.send(.openURL(url))`.

If the link contains full credentials, Home first shows "Connect to “name”?".
`DeepLinkConfirmation` describes the endpoint, saved or new status, connection
script, TLS, and redirect. Only **Connect** calls
`DatabaseListViewModel.connect(_:onOpen:)`. Other links open
`DatabaseFormView(deepLink:)`, which does not connect automatically.

**Cancel** calls `onDeepLinkCancelled`. AppShell then removes the pending
redirect. AppShell keeps an accepted link's `redirect` in `DeepLinkRedirects`.
It navigates to that page when the database window opens.

### Database window controls

The window uses a two-column `NavigationSplitView`. `DatabaseSidebar` groups
Data, Analytics, and Messaging, with ⌘1 through ⌘7 in sidebar order.
Selection maps to `navigator.section` and the analytics tab.

The detail column places section content above the bottom panel for CLI and
Profiler (⇧⌘Y). The `.inspector` hosts Command Helper, Tips, and Tutorials
(⌥⌘I). The toolbar orders its controls as follows:

1. The database switcher popover (⌘O).
2. The database name as title and section with `host:port` as subtitle.
3. The principal activity area. It shows the production label followed by
   connection status or metrics. Clicking it opens database info.
4. The section's `.primaryAction` items.
5. The logical database menu if the server has more than one database.
6. The bottom panel and inspector toggles.

SwiftUI orders `.primaryAction` items by view-tree order. AppShell declares
trailing items on a zero-height view after section content. A pushed
`NavigationStack` page hides those items, so sections switch pages in place.

The section and bottom panel share one SwiftUI stack with a drag divider.
The panel height persists in `settings.ui.bottomPanelSizes`. Keep a 1 pt
inset from sidebar, toolbar, and inspector edges. Without it, AppKit split
views (`HSplitView` and `VSplitView`) expand into those safe-area insets.
`FixedPane` constrains each section and panel to its offered space.
A feature's minimum width therefore cannot push other columns outside the window.

The menu bar accesses the key window through the `databaseSession` focused
scene value and feature command values. These include `homeCommands`,
`browserCommands`, `workbenchCommands`, `editorRunAction`, `analyticsCommands`,
and `profilerCommands`. AppShell's `MenuShortcut` table owns key equivalents.
`AppShellTests` checks it against SettingsFeature's `ShortcutCatalog`.

**Edit > Find > Find…** (⌘F) focuses Browser or Home search. In other sections,
it opens the focused text view's find bar. With `--open`,
`--sheet edit-database|tags` opens Edit Database or `ManageTagsSheet` in the
database window.

### Launch flags for checks and screenshots

Use these flags to select the initial app state:

- `--open host:port[/db]` creates or updates a database named `host:port` and
  opens it. `--open-id <id>` opens a saved database.
- `--home` also shows Home. `--add-database` opens Add Database.
- `--section browser|workbench|vectorsearch|analysis|slowlog|cluster|pubsub`
  selects a section.
- `--tool cli|profiler|helper` opens a tool. You may repeat it.
  `helper` opens Command Helper in the inspector.
- `--inspector helper|tips|tutorials` selects an inspector pane.
- `--key <name>` selects a Browser key and implies `--section browser`.
- `--settings [general|workbench|advanced]` opens Settings.
- `--shortcuts` opens Keyboard Shortcuts.
- `--appearance light|dark` overrides appearance for one run.
- `--window-size WxH` sets the first window's size.
- `--sheet <name>` populates `LaunchOptions.sheet` for features to consume.

Any flag makes Keystrand ignore saved window state for that run.
`scripts/snapshot.sh <out.png> [flags]` starts with a fresh
`KEYSTRAND_DATA_DIR` and captures the frontmost window.

### Overview metrics

The session refreshes `session.overview` every 5 seconds while its window is
visible, based on `windowDidAppear` and `windowDidDisappear`.
`connect()` and `reconnect()` restart polling if the window is visible.
`disconnect()` stops polling. Errors go to `session.overviewError`.

SettingsFeature's `OverviewBarView` renders compact metric text. It also runs
a timer from `settings.ui.autoRefreshEnabled` and `autoRefreshRates["overview"]`
through `OverviewRefreshPreferences`. The **Refresh** section of
`DatabaseInfoPopover` edits those settings.

### Live tips and automatic refresh

Features report triggers through `session.liveTips`, a RedisCore `LiveTipSignals`.
These sources report live tips:

- `KeyScanner` reports `useSmallerKeys`.
- `KeyMetadataService.metadata` reports `searchJSON` after one `FT._LIST` check
  per session. `.keyInfo` reports `bigSets`, `bigStrings`, and `compressionForList`.
- Hash loading reports `bigHashes`. String loading reports `stringToJson`.
- Sorted set loading reports `RTS`. CLI commands report `searchVisualization`.
- Logical database switches report `avoidLogicalDatabases`.

`LiveTipRules` defines thresholds. AnalyticsFeature's `LiveTipsModel` installs
its handler in `load()` and stores each tip once per database. Signals buffer
until the handler is ready.

RedisUI `AutoRefreshMenu` combines Refresh with an automatic refresh menu.
`AutoRefreshMenu(persistsEnabledState: false)` keeps enabled state in the view,
as Slow Log requires. `isPaused: true` skips timer ticks.

## 6. Feature entry points

AppShell composes the features through these public views. Keep their signatures stable; add parameters only with
default values.

| Target | Files | Public signature |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ConnectionsFeature | `Sources/Features/ConnectionsFeature/HomeView.swift` | `HomeView(onOpenDatabase: @escaping @MainActor (DatabaseConfig) -> Void, onDatabasesDeleted: @escaping @MainActor ([DatabaseID]) -> Void = { _ in }, autoDiscovery: LocalAutoDiscovery? = LocalAutoDiscovery.appDefault)` |
| | `Sources/Features/ConnectionsFeature/DatabaseFormView.swift` | `enum DatabaseFormMode { case add; case edit(DatabaseConfig); case clone(DatabaseConfig) }`; `DatabaseFormView(mode:, focusEnvironment: Bool = false, onSaved: @escaping @MainActor (DatabaseConfig) -> Void = { _ in })` and `DatabaseFormView(deepLink:onSaved:)` (Add Database prefilled from a link), presented as a sheet, dismisses itself |
| | `Sources/Features/ConnectionsFeature/DatabasePickerPopover.swift` | `DatabasePickerPopover(currentDatabaseID: DatabaseID?, onSelect: @escaping @MainActor (DatabaseConfig) -> Void, onOpenHome: @escaping @MainActor () -> Void = {})` |
| BrowserFeature | `Sources/Features/BrowserFeature/BrowserView.swift` | `BrowserView(session: DatabaseSession, keyDetails: @escaping @MainActor (KeyDetailsContext) -> AnyView, addKeyForm: @escaping @MainActor (KeyType, AddKeyFormContext) -> AnyView? = { _, _ in nil })` |
| KeyTypesBasicFeature | `KeyDetailsHeaderView.swift`, `StringKeyView.swift`, `HashKeyView.swift`, `ListKeyView.swift`, `SetKeyView.swift` in `Sources/Features/KeyTypesBasicFeature/` | each `init(context: KeyDetailsContext)` |
| KeyTypesAdvancedFeature | `ZSetKeyView.swift`, `StreamKeyView.swift`, `JSONKeyView.swift`, `VectorSetKeyView.swift`, `TimeSeriesKeyView.swift`, `ArrayKeyView.swift`, `UnsupportedKeyView.swift` in `Sources/Features/KeyTypesAdvancedFeature/` | each `init(context: KeyDetailsContext)` (graph and unknown types go to `UnsupportedKeyView`, which also takes `note: String? = nil`) |
| | `Sources/Features/KeyTypesAdvancedFeature/VectorSetAddKeyForm.swift` | `VectorSetAddKeyForm(context: AddKeyFormContext)` |
| WorkbenchFeature | `Sources/Features/WorkbenchFeature/WorkbenchView.swift`, `TutorialsPanelView.swift` | `WorkbenchView(session:)`, `TutorialsPanelView(session:)` (Insights > Tutorials; run code through `session.navigator.navigate(.workbench(script:))`) |
| CLIFeature | `Sources/Features/CLIFeature/CLIView.swift`, `CommandHelperView.swift` | `CLIView(session:)`, `CommandHelperView(session:)`; share CLI and Helper state with `session.scopedObject` |
| AnalyticsFeature | `Sources/Features/AnalyticsFeature/DatabaseAnalysisView.swift`, `SlowLogView.swift`, `ClusterDetailsView.swift`, `TipsPanelView.swift` | each `init(session:)`; AppShell shows the tab picker, and Cluster Details only for clusters |
| MonitoringFeature | `Sources/Features/MonitoringFeature/PubSubView.swift`, `ProfilerView.swift` | `PubSubView(session:)`, `ProfilerView(session:, onClose: (@MainActor () -> Void)? = nil)`, `ProfilerView.isRunning(session:)`, `ProfilerView.close(session:) async`; keep view models in `session.scopedObject` |
| VectorSearchFeature | `Sources/Features/VectorSearchFeature/VectorSearchView.swift` | `VectorSearchView(session:)` |
| SettingsFeature | `Sources/Features/SettingsFeature/SettingsView.swift`, `OverviewBarView.swift`, `DatabaseInfoPopover.swift`, `KeyboardShortcutsView.swift`, `AcknowledgementsView.swift` | `SettingsView()`, `OverviewBarView(session:)`, `DatabaseInfoPopover(session:)`, `KeyboardShortcutsView()`, `AcknowledgementsView()` |

The smoke tests in `Tests/<Feature>Tests/<Feature>SmokeTests.swift` construct these views.

## 7. Build and test commands

Run everything from the repository root. `swift test` builds every test target into one bundle, so a compile error in any
target blocks all tests.

```bash
# Whole package, or one target and its dependencies
swift build --scratch-path .build/dev
swift build --scratch-path .build/dev --target BrowserFeature

# All tests, or one test target
swift test --scratch-path .build/dev
swift test --scratch-path .build/dev --filter BrowserFeatureTests
swift test --scratch-path .build/dev --filter "RESPTests|RedisClientTests"

# Debug app
xcodegen generate
xcodebuild -project Keystrand.xcodeproj -scheme Keystrand -destination 'platform=macOS' \
  -derivedDataPath .build/dd build
open .build/dd/Build/Products/Debug/Keystrand.app

# Tools, Release install, version
scripts/bootstrap.sh        # Xcode check, Brewfile tools, git hooks
scripts/install.sh --open   # Release build, sign, install, launch
scripts/version.sh          # version from the nearest v* tag, else 0.0.0-dev

# Generated files (CI fails when one is stale)
scripts/check-generated.sh  # command specs, sample data, icon, strings, API reference
scripts/make-api-reference.sh
```

Launch the app against a throwaway store with `KEYSTRAND_DATA_DIR=<dir>`; `scripts/snapshot.sh <out.png> [launch args]` does this with a fresh temporary directory (set `APP=` to capture a Debug build).

The feature target and test target names are `ConnectionsFeature`/`ConnectionsFeatureTests`,
`BrowserFeature`, `KeyTypesBasicFeature`, `KeyTypesAdvancedFeature`, `WorkbenchFeature`, `CLIFeature`,
`AnalyticsFeature`, `MonitoringFeature`, `VectorSearchFeature`, and `SettingsFeature`, each with a
`…Tests` test target.

## 8. Tests and the live server

`TestSupport.RedisTestServer` provides these helpers:

| Helper | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `host` / `port` | `127.0.0.1` / `6390` |
| `isAvailable` | One TCP probe per process (always true with `KEYSTRAND_REQUIRE_LIVE=1`, always false with `KEYSTRAND_SKIP_LIVE=1`) |
| `requiresLive` | `KEYSTRAND_REQUIRE_LIVE=1` is set: every `is…Available` flag is true, so a missing server fails its tests instead of skipping them |
| `skipsLive` | `KEYSTRAND_SKIP_LIVE=1` is set: every live test is disabled without probing (CI's unit-test job) |
| `live(_:modules:)` | Gate for a live suite or test: `.redis`, `.valkey`, or `.valkeyCluster` is reachable and loads `modules`; always paired with `.tags(.live)` |
| `needs(_:on:)` | Extra gate for one test of a live suite that needs a module the rest of the suite does not |
| `needs(_:on:)` with `Feature` / `hasFeatures(_:on:)` | Gate on a command older servers lack, probed once per process with `COMMAND INFO`; `Feature` is `.hashFieldTTL` (`HEXPIRE`), `.array` (`ARSET`) |
| `hasModules(_:on:)` / `modules(at:)` | `MODULE LIST` of a server, probed once per process; `Module` is `.search`, `.json`, `.timeSeries`, `.bloom`, `.vectorSet` |
| `DB.*` | Index constants (one per test target, `search = 0`) |
| `connectionConfig(db:endpoint:)` | Connection config for a test db |
| `databaseConfig(db:name:endpoint:)` | Saved-database model for a test db |
| `makeConnection(db:endpoint:)` | Opens a connection |
| `flush(db:)` | Refuses db 0 |
| `freshConnection(db:endpoint:)` | Opens a connection on a flushed db |
| `uniquePrefix(_:)` | Unique key prefix |
| `makeServices()` | Temp-dir `AppServices`, in-memory secrets |
| `mockSession(_:config:)` | Session backed by a scripted client |
| `liveSession(db:name:endpoint:configure:)` | Real connected `DatabaseSession` on the test server (config saved in temp services) |
| `endpoint` | `127.0.0.1:6390`; the default `endpoint:` of the connection, config, and session helpers |
| `valkey` / `isValkeyAvailable` | Valkey 9 bundle at `127.0.0.1:6392` (container `keystrand-valkey`) and its probe; pass `endpoint: RedisTestServer.valkey` to the helpers, with the same `DB.*` indexes |
| `valkeyCluster` / `isValkeyClusterAvailable` | Valkey 9 cluster at `127.0.0.1:7001-7003` (`cluster-databases 4`, containers `keystrand-vkc-0/1/2`); the probe checks `CLUSTER INFO` for `cluster_state:ok` |
| `valkeyClusterDatabaseConfig(db:)` | Saved cluster config on that cluster (only DBs 0-3 exist); open it with `liveSession(config:)` |
| `liveSession(config:)` | Real connected `DatabaseSession` for any saved config |
| `ValkeyFixtures.serverInfo` / `.capabilities` | Valkey 9.1.2 INFO lines and capabilities (json, bf, search modules) for scripted tests |
| `emptyDatabase(_:)` | SCAN + UNLINK every key of a client's current database |
| `seedAllTypes(_:prefix:)` | One key of every type (string, hash, list, set, zset, stream, JSON, time series, vector set) |
| `seedStrings(_:prefix:count:)` | `count` string keys, pipelined |
| `deleteKeys(_:prefix:)` | Removes `prefix*` keys (DB 0 cleanup) |

**Never send `FLUSHDB` / `FLUSHALL` from tests.** RediSearch drops every index on any flush event, even a `FLUSHDB`
of another logical database, which breaks search tests running at the same time in DB 0. `flush(db:)` and
`freshConnection(db:)` therefore delete keys with SCAN + UNLINK.

Live tests carry the `live` tag and must skip when the server is down or lacks a module they use. SwiftPM cannot filter
by tag, so `RedisTestServer.live(...)` also honors `KEYSTRAND_SKIP_LIVE=1`; CI checks that every such gate has the tag:

```swift
@Test(.tags(.live), RedisTestServer.live(.redis)) func scansKeys() async throws {
    let connection = try await RedisTestServer.freshConnection(db: RedisTestServer.DB.browser)
    // ...
}
```

Use `MockRedisClient` for logic that does not need a server. Do not connect to `127.0.0.1:6379`. A developer's local Redis can use that address.

`scripts/test-servers.sh` starts every container the live tests expect: `keystrand-test` (Redis 8, 6390),
`keystrand-valkey` (Valkey 9 bundle, 6392) and the Valkey cluster `keystrand-vkc-0/1/2` (7001-7003, host networking,
created with `valkey-cli --cluster create` on first start). `scripts/test-servers.sh stop valkey` and
`stop cluster` stop the extras again; `status` lists all five. `scripts/test-servers.sh native` runs the
same servers as Homebrew `redis-server` (with the modules from Homebrew's `opt/redis`) and `valkey-server` processes
(no modules), with pid files and logs in `.build/test-servers/`; CI uses it because GitHub's macOS runners have no
Docker. `KEYSTRAND_REDIS_SERVER`, `KEYSTRAND_VALKEY_SERVER`, `KEYSTRAND_REDIS_CLI`, `KEYSTRAND_VALKEY_CLI`, and
`KEYSTRAND_REDIS_MODULES` pick other binaries and modules.

| Test target | DB index |
| --- | --- |
| RESPTests | 1 |
| RedisClientTests | 2 |
| RedisCoreTests | 3 (and 15, `DB.redisCoreSpare`, for tests that move a connection to another database) |
| BrowserFeatureTests | 4 |
| KeyTypesBasicFeatureTests | 5 |
| KeyTypesAdvancedFeatureTests | 6 |
| WorkbenchFeatureTests | 7 |
| CLIFeatureTests | 8 |
| AnalyticsFeatureTests | 9 |
| MonitoringFeatureTests | 10 |
| VectorSearchFeatureTests | 11 (search/vector tests: DB 0 with a unique prefix and unique index name; drop the index after the test) |
| ConnectionsFeatureTests | 12 (auto-discovery must use injected ports; never probe 6379) |
| SettingsFeatureTests | 13 |
| RedisUITests | 14 |

## 9. Known gaps to watch

Account for these limits when you change the code:

- **RediSearch and flushes.** Redis 8.10 drops every search index after any
  flush, including `FLUSHDB` on another logical database. Tests must not flush
  databases, as specified in section 8. CLI and Workbench ask for confirmation
  before `FLUSHDB` or `FLUSHALL`.
- **Production confirmation.** `CommandExecutor` does not enforce confirmation.
  Workbench, the Vector Search query page, and CLI must call `session.confirm(...)`
  for commands in `session.dangerousCommands`. Key views call
  `context.confirmProduction(_:)`. Workbench and Vector Search use action IDs
  `dangerous:<VERB>`. For several verbs, they use `dangerous:A,B`.
  A skip covers each listed verb within those features. They use the button
  title "Run command". CLI uses `redis-command:<VERB>`, so skips do not transfer
  between CLI and those features. Vector Search, Workbench, and CLI also
  confirm `FLUSHDB` and `FLUSHALL` on every database.
- **History writes.** If a history write fails, `CommandExecutor.run` keeps
  replies and returns executions with `isNotStored = true`. It passes the
  first error to `onPersistError`. Later chunks still run.
- **Workbench timing.** Every result in a pipeline chunk shows the chunk's
  round-trip time because pipelined replies arrive together.
- **Search paging.** `SearchIndexService.searchKeys` throws
  `SearchIndexError.maxResultsTooLow(offset + limit, setting:)` when `offset`
  reaches the server cap. Redis uses `MAXSEARCHRESULTS`. valkey-search uses
  `search.max-nonvector-search-results-fetched`. The `setting` argument names
  the applicable setting. The last allowed page returns `nextOffset == nil`.
  valkey-search has no match-all query. For an empty query, Keystrand searches
  the range of the first indexed NUMERIC attribute whose name has no whitespace.
  The query grammar cannot reference names with whitespace. If no suitable
  attribute exists, the method throws `SearchIndexError.queryRequired`.
- **Pub/Sub subscriptions.** A new subscription on an already subscribed
  connection replaces its stream. The previous channels remain subscribed.
  Their messages go to the new stream. Use one dedicated connection per subscription.
- **Sentinel over SSH.** The factory connects directly to Sentinel nodes.
  It does not tunnel those connections.
- **Live topology coverage.** Redis cluster routing uses `MockRedisClient`
  tests. Sentinel has parser tests only. The optional Valkey 9 cluster,
  `RedisTestServer.valkeyCluster`, covers numbered databases, scans, and `MOVED`.
  Its tests skip if the cluster is unavailable. SSH tunnels have no end-to-end
  test. The cluster-only Analytics Overview tab was checked only against
  standalone servers, where it redirects to Database Analysis.
- **Formatters.** ZSTD frames that need a dictionary return
  `DecompressorError.unsupported(.zstd)`. The decoder does not check ZSTD
  xxHash64 or Snappy CRC32C checksums. It decodes only the first member of a
  multi-member GZIP stream.
- **Editor and completion.** RediSearch query sub-token completion supports
  `@field` templates and APPLY functions. The editor does not collapse vector
  embeddings in the glyph margin or color RediSearch sub-tokens separately.
  It skips syntax highlighting above 400k UTF-16 units.
- **TextKit 1 control characters.** `CodeEditorView` and CLI output use
  `EditorLayoutManager` to lay out vertical tab and form feed as whitespace.
  Neither starts a new line. TextKit 1's default line-break and container-break
  actions can prevent layout from finishing after a resize. This occurs if a
  combining mark, joiner, or variation selector follows either character.
  Build new TextKit 1 text views on `EditorLayoutManager`.
- **RESP3 attributes.** The parser reads and discards attributes.
- **Client certificate keys.** `PEMSecurity` builds TLS `SecIdentity` values
  in memory with `SecKeyCreateWithData` and `SecIdentityCreate`. It never writes
  to a keychain. It supports RSA keys in PKCS#1 or PKCS#8, and EC keys on
  P-256, P-384, and P-521 in SEC1 or PKCS#8. EC keys require named curves.
  Encrypted keys and certificates with explicit EC curve parameters return
  `PEMSecurityError`.
- **Dedicated connections.** `DatabaseSession` tracks connections from
  `makeDedicatedConnection` and `makeDedicatedNodeConnections`.
  `disconnect()`, `reconnect()`, and `close()` notify every scoped object through
  `sessionConnectionDidReset()` before closing those connections.
  `apply(config:)` does the same if the connection signature changes.
  Rename, environment, or password-only edits leave connections open.
  Bulk upload connections are untracked, so an upload during a host edit
  finishes against the previous server.
- **Reconnects.** A dropped socket leaves session state at `.connected`.
  AppShell's `WindowConnectionModel` detects the loss after overview polling
  and `PING` both fail. It then offers Retry. Cluster SSH tunnels do not restart
  if their SSH processes exit. Cluster node clients do not rerun the
  pre-connection script on reconnect.
- **Browser scroll restoration.** Each search mode keeps its scroll position
  through `ScrollViewReader.scrollTo` on a SwiftUI `List` with short delays.
  Unit tests cover only anchor bookkeeping.
- **Window visibility.** Cluster Details, Slow Log, and the overview bar use
  `NSWindow.occlusionState`. Unit tests cover only the pure visibility rule.
- **AppKit layout warning.** At launch, AppKit can log
  "It's not legal to call -layoutSubtreeIfNeeded on a view which is already being laid out".
  The likely source is an AppKit-backed `HSplitView` or `VSplitView`.
  The warning has no visible effect.
- **Manual launches.** First-run discovery on an empty database list probes
  every local listening port, including `127.0.0.1:6379`. Before manual checks,
  set `KEYSTRAND_DISABLE_AUTODISCOVERY=1`. Test runners and launches with
  `--open` or `--open-id` disable the scan.

## 10. Status

The last full check used macOS 26.6 with Xcode 27. It recorded these results:

- `swift build` of the whole package in Swift 6 language mode: zero errors and zero
  warnings. No `@unchecked Sendable` or `nonisolated(unsafe)` without a comment saying why it is safe.
- `swift test`: 1,270 tests in 17 test targets, all passing and none skipped, against the live server at 127.0.0.1:6390.
- `xcodegen generate` and a Release `xcodebuild`, copied with `ditto` to `dist/Keystrand.app` (universal arm64 + x86_64,
  ad-hoc signed). The Release app launches, connects to 127.0.0.1:6390 and renders every section.
- A representative gallery of the Release app in dark mode (`docs/screenshots`, embedded in `README.md`): Home, Add
  Database, Browser tree, Hash key details, Workbench, CLI drawer, Database Analysis and Settings. Every key type, the
  inspector panes, Slow Log, Pub/Sub, Profiler, Vector Search and Keyboard Shortcuts were also captured in dark and
  light and checked, but are not kept in the repository.
- `grep -rn 'RedisInsight' Sources` matches only the RedisInsight import and export format code and its file-picker
  footer. No hex colors exist in `Sources`; the only `hex:` matches are `case .hex:` labels
  of the HEX value and key-name formats.
- `keystrand://` links were not opened from the OS; the parser, the Home handling and the redirect queue are unit-tested.

Use these commands for a smoke test:

```bash
KEYSTRAND_DISABLE_AUTODISCOVERY=1 dist/Keystrand.app/Contents/MacOS/Keystrand --open 127.0.0.1:6390[/db] [--section …]
scripts/snapshot.sh /tmp/shot.png --open 127.0.0.1:6390/4 --key demo:user:1001 --appearance dark
```

`--open host:port[/db]` upserts a database named `host:port` (it updates `db` when it differs) and opens its window.
`scripts/snapshot.sh` uses a fresh `KEYSTRAND_DATA_DIR` unless one is set; the gallery used a data directory with four
saved databases and DB 4 seeded with `demo:*` keys of every type (`docker exec keystrand-test redis-cli -n 4 …`).

Use these commands for a Release build:

```bash
xcodebuild -project Keystrand.xcodeproj -scheme Keystrand -configuration Release -destination 'platform=macOS' \
  -derivedDataPath .build/dd build
ditto .build/dd/Build/Products/Release/Keystrand.app dist/Keystrand.app
```

| Contract item | State |
| --- | --- |
| RESP, RedisClient, Formatters, CommandSpecs, RedisCore | Real. Limits are in section 9. |
| RedisUI | Real: the DESIGN.md §4 component kit, `EditorAppearance`, `SheetForm`, status and confirmation modifiers, `QALaunchOptions`. |
| AppShell | Real: Home `Window`, database `WindowGroup` with sidebar, unified toolbar and activity area, inspector (Command Helper, Tips, Tutorials), bottom panel (CLI, Profiler), Settings, Keyboard Shortcuts and Acknowledgements scenes, the full menu bar (DESIGN.md §8), launch flags (§9.1), app icon. |
| ConnectionsFeature: `HomeView`, `DatabaseFormView`, `DatabasePickerPopover` | Real |
| BrowserFeature: `BrowserView` | Real |
| KeyTypesBasicFeature: `KeyDetailsHeaderView`, `StringKeyView`, `HashKeyView`, `ListKeyView`, `SetKeyView` | Real |
| KeyTypesAdvancedFeature: `ZSetKeyView`, `StreamKeyView`, `JSONKeyView`, `VectorSetKeyView`, `TimeSeriesKeyView`, `ArrayKeyView`, `UnsupportedKeyView`, `VectorSetAddKeyForm` | Real |
| WorkbenchFeature: `WorkbenchView`, `TutorialsPanelView` | Real |
| CLIFeature: `CLIView`, `CommandHelperView` | Real. The CLI reconnects when an edit changes its connection fingerprint (host, port, db, user, TLS, SSH, sentinel, type). |
| AnalyticsFeature: `DatabaseAnalysisView`, `SlowLogView`, `ClusterDetailsView`, `TipsPanelView` | Real |
| MonitoringFeature: `PubSubView`, `ProfilerView` | Real |
| VectorSearchFeature: `VectorSearchView` | Real |
| SettingsFeature: `SettingsView`, `OverviewBarView`, `DatabaseInfoPopover`, `KeyboardShortcutsView`, `AcknowledgementsView` | Real |
| Feature tests | View-model, logic, and live tests per feature (section 8 DB indexes), plus `AppShellTests` |

`Sources/` and `Tests/` contain no `TODO` or `FIXME` markers. The app does not use `NotImplementedView`.

### Deviations from DESIGN.md

| Area | Deviation | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Tables | Column widths of the key list and key details tables are not persisted | SwiftUI `Table` exposes no column widths |
| Browser | The key list and key details open at an even split | `HSplitView` ignores `idealWidth` |
| Vector Search | Pages are routed in place, not on a `NavigationStack` | A pushed `NavigationStack` page hid AppShell's toolbar items (decision 11) |
| Pub/Sub | No jump-to-latest button | Not built; the CLI and Profiler have `JumpToLatestButton` |
| Tables | Column descriptions are tooltips on cells, not headers | SwiftUI `TableColumn` headers cannot carry their own tooltips |
| Vector Search | Save Query failures surface as an error alert from the status center, not inline in the sheet | The sheet keeps its fields; the error is not wired to `SheetForm.errorMessage` |
| QA | No screenshots or live tests of Cluster, Sentinel, SSH or Redis Software | No such server is available |
| Database window | The bottom panel is a SwiftUI stack with a `DragGesture` divider (`DrawerDivider`), not a `VSplitView` (§2.3.5, §4.5) | An AppKit split view in the detail column lays out under the floating sidebar |

### Not implemented or partly implemented

| Feature | Item | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| KeyTypesBasicFeature | Per-database binary Value Decoder rules for hash values; special handling of very long key names | Out of scope |
| KeyTypesBasicFeature | Header auto refresh shown as paused while a value reloads | `KeyDetailsState` has no loading flag; the type views already skip refreshes while loading |
| KeyTypesBasicFeature | Redis 7.4 hash field-TTL path | Tested only by forcing the strategy on the 8.10 server |
| KeyTypesAdvancedFeature | RedisJSON v1 legacy paths | Implemented; checked only against a mock client because the test server runs RedisJSON 8 |
| KeyTypesAdvancedFeature | Paged fetch of huge JSON containers | `JSONTreeModel` has no partly loaded container node; children of a big container are summarized all at once, built 2,000 per pipeline chunk |
| WorkbenchFeature | Vector-embedding collapse in the editor | Needs fold or glyph support in `CodeEditorView` |
| WorkbenchFeature | Geo visualizations (map, heatmap, geoshape) for FT.SEARCH / FT.AGGREGATE / FT.HYBRID results | Not implemented; GEO* commands have map, heatmap and details views |
| MonitoringFeature, CLIFeature | Graceful UI reset through `SessionScopedObject.sessionConnectionDidReset()` | RedisCore force-closes dedicated connections on disconnect and on a connection edit; Pub/Sub and Profiler learn about it when their stream ends |
| VectorSearchFeature | Embedding collapse chips in the query editor | Needs `CodeEditorView` support; the editor runs without line wrapping |
| VectorSearchFeature | Onboarding tour anchors | Header frames are not exposed; tour steps 3-5 point at evenly spaced spots over the table |
| RedisCore | Shared key-tree builder | BrowserFeature `KeyTreeBuilder` and VectorSearchFeature `PickerKeyTree` are duplicates |
| RedisUI | Shared window-visibility reader | AnalyticsFeature and SettingsFeature each keep an internal `WindowVisibility` copy |
| RedisCore | Binary `patternData` on `BrowserNavigationRequest` | Analytics uses `?` per non-printable byte, which can match a few extra keys |
| AppShell | Zoom In / Out / Reset | Not implemented; there is no app-wide zoom factor |
| SettingsFeature | Workbench "result limits" setting | `AppSettings` has no result-limit field; needs a RedisCore change |

The following implemented features also need checks when related code changes:

- Key details provide automatic refresh by key type. `KeyDetailsComposer`
  composes the header and type view. JSON edits disable header refresh.
- Browser supports wheel-triggered scanning, scroll positions for each search
  mode, and a Back control in narrow windows. It also supports the RediSearch
  index picker, `MAXSEARCHRESULTS` paging, and missing-module sheets.
- Array provides a non-Unicode edit guard and confirmation to save as Unicode.
  It also provides caret-aware filter completion and a debounced `VSIM` preview.
- Workbench supports a right Y axis with key assignments and independent axis
  scales. Collapsed results show `Matched:N`. Tutorials restore scroll positions.
  `GEO*` commands provide maps, heatmaps, and details.
- Analytics includes the "No Expiry" divider and graphdata length. Cluster
  Details and Slow Log skip timer ticks while the window is hidden.
- Vector Search includes a key tree picker, onboarding tour, and popovers.
- Home, Edit Database, and database info display the hosting provider.
  Connection links use the `keystrand://` scheme.
- AppShell titles database windows as `name[ [dbN]]` with section and `host:port`
  as the subtitle. Analytics pages use sidebar rows. Cluster Details appears
  only for clusters. The Database Analysis row shows the unread Tips count.
- Workbench runs disable the logical database menu. The Profiler's running
  state appears in the bottom panel and menu. The panel height persists.
- Sessions close on quit and reconnect after relevant edits. Pub/Sub and
  `MONITOR` connections use a 25-second timeout without retries.
- AppShell presents the production prompt with a suppression checkbox.
  **Database > Edit Database…** opens the edit form. Key edits use plain
  confirmations. Every feature can report live-tip triggers.
- Lost connections show **Reconnect**. Disconnected sessions show **Connect**.

---

This section is generated by `scripts/make-api-reference.sh` from
`swift package dump-symbol-graph --minimum-access-level public`. Do not edit it
by hand. After you add, remove, or change a `public` symbol, run the script.

Every symbol listed is `public` (or `open`). Each subsection is one file, in
layer order. Members are indented under their type in source order.
`extension X` groups members declared in an extension whose type lives in
another file, or in a constrained extension (`where ...`). Implied
conformances (`Copyable`, `Escapable`, `SendableMetatype`) are left out, and
the conformances of a type are sorted by name.

enum ConnectionKind: Equatable, Hashable, RawRepresentable, Sendable

    case cluster

@MainActor protocol SessionScopedObject : AnyObject

    func sessionConnectionDidReset() async
extension SessionScopedObject
    func sessionConnectionDidReset() async
@MainActor final class DatabaseSession: Identifiable, Observable, Sendable
    nonisolated let id: DatabaseID

    var capabilities: DatabaseCapabilities { get set }
    var connectionKind: ConnectionKind { get set }
    var dbIndex: Int { get set }
    var databaseCount: Int { get set }
    var dbGeneration: Int { get set }

    var dangerousCommands: Set<String> { get set }
    let navigator: DatabaseNavigator
    let toasts: ToastCenter
    let confirmations: ConfirmationCenter
    let liveTips: LiveTipSignals
    let services: AppServices
    var overviewRefreshInterval: TimeInterval
    init(config: DatabaseConfig, services: AppServices, client: (any RedisClient)? = nil)
    var environment: DatabaseEnvironment { get }
    var isProduction: Bool { get }
    var isCluster: Bool { get }
    var effectiveDB: Int { get }
    var serverVersion: String? { get }
    var serverFlavor: ServerFlavor { get }
    var username: String? { get }
    var confirmationName: String { get }
    func connect() async
    func disconnect() async
    func reconnect() async
    func close() async
    func apply(config newConfig: DatabaseConfig) async
    func refreshInfo() async throws
    func canSelect(db: Int) async -> Bool
    func select(db: Int) async throws
    func client(context: ClientContext = .common) async throws -> any RedisClient
    nonisolated static func commandTimeout(for context: ClientContext) -> TimeInterval?
    func ping(timeout: TimeInterval) async throws
    func execute(_ args: [RedisArgument], context: ClientContext = .common) async throws -> RESPValue
    func execute(_ args: RedisArgument...) async throws -> RESPValue
    func execute(_ args: [Data], context: ClientContext = .common) async throws -> RESPValue
    func pipeline(_ commands: [[RedisArgument]], context: ClientContext = .common) async throws -> [RESPValue]
    func pipeline(_ commands: [[Data]], context: ClientContext = .common) async throws -> [RESPValue]
    func nodes(role: NodeRole? = nil, context: ClientContext = .common) async throws -> [NodeClient]
    func makeDedicatedClient(context: ClientContext = .common) async throws -> any RedisClient
    func makeDedicatedConnection(node: RedisEndpoint? = nil, context: ClientContext = .common, connectTimeout: TimeInterval? = nil, retry: RetryPolicy? = nil) async throws -> RedisConnection
    func makeDedicatedNodeConnections(role: NodeRole? = nil, context: ClientContext = .common) async throws -> [RedisConnection]
    func confirm(_ request: ConfirmationRequest) async -> Bool
    func scopedObject<T>(_ type: T.Type = T.self, make: () -> T) -> T where T : AnyObject
    func windowDidAppear()
    func windowDidDisappear()
